#519. Fleetwood Town v Arsenal: Carabao Cup fourth round – live

⚽ League Cup updates from the 8pm GMT kick-off ⚽ Cod Army welcome Gunners to Highbury | Mail Niall

12 mins: Fleetwood launch a long throw into the danger area, where it’s flicked on by Bennett – but Arsenal get it clear.

The drone has now disappeared, so the referee opts to restart. There are also murky reports of the culprit being apprehended by police. So, it’s back to the game, which will have at least eight minutes added to the first half.

“Who’s the wanker with the drone?” bellow the 5,000-strong home faithful, while the referee talks to Mikel Arteta, Matt Lawlor and various security staff. And now he’s waving the players back to the tunnel!

Arsenal get upfield early on and have a free kick … but play is suspended as the referee, Michael Salisbury, peers up at a distant light in the sky. There’s a drone hovering over Highbury, and it’ll need to be dealt with somehow.

On grounds with the same(ish) name, Tim Gutteridge writes: “I give you Liverpool (Anfield) and Stirling Albion who, until 1993, played their home games at Annfield (with an extra N).”

“The similarities don’t stop there. For much of their history, Stirling Albion played in an all-red strip. And between 1970 and 1973 they were managed by Bob Shankly, the older brother of Liverpool’s Bill.”

Arsenal kick off, in their navy away threads with red and yellow strip. Fleetwood’s home kit is an Arsenal-esque red number with white sleeves.

The players are out on the pitch, in a fug of firework smoke after some very rapid-fire pyro greeted their arrival. Peter Drury, never knowingly understated, is the Sky commentator and is already talking nights with the stars, marble halls and “two teams galaxies apart”. Let’s go!

“I like to root for the underdog, so fingers crossed the Fishermen of the Cod Army will catch a really big one today!” cheers Peter Oh. It may interest you, Peter, that Fleetwood have a sub called Andy Cannon – surely destined to come on and sink the Gunners.

“‘Arsenal have paid us a lot of respect by naming such a strong team’?Not sure why Matt Lawlor’s being so polite, Arteta’s taking the proverbial Michael with that side. Three kids and a goalie on debut?” writes Dave Estherby. To be fair, one of those kids is Max Dowman, and there are a few first-team regulars in there.

Here’s the Fleetwood head coach, Matt Lawlor: “We’ve had to make changes, with the champions in town. But we’ve got a plan with substitutions to manage the game. Arsenal have paid us a lot of respect by naming such a strong team … it’s a very difficult challenge, but one we should enjoy, and not be overawed.”

“I was just thinking, with the two Highburys – I can only think of the St James(’) Parks (Exeter and Newcastle), but are there any other grounds with very similar names?” asks Shaun T.

“It’s very special, coming to these kinds of grounds – it’s proper English football,” says Mikel Arteta. “In these kinds of conditions, you’re in or you’re out. It’s great to have Marli, Max and Ceadach with us, Ilhan Meslier as well who is making his first appearance for us.” His advice to those young Gunners? “Give me a headache, give me a problem – give me a reason to play you.”

There is one other fourth-round Carabao Cup tie tonight, and it’s an all-League One affair. Bradford City, finalists in 2013, host Robbie Savage’s Peterborough – guaranteeing that at least one third-tier side will make the quarter-finals. Like the match at Highbury, it will go to penalties if still level after 90 minutes – and there’s no VAR.

So, Ilhan Meslier makes his debut in goal for Arsenal, with Christian Mosquera and Piero Hincapie starting as they return from injury; Martin Zubimendi also gets a run-out, while youngsters Max Dowman, Marli Salmon and Ceadach O’Neill all included in Mikel Arteta’s starting XI.

Fleetwood Town (3-5-2): Lynch, Haughey, Potter, Bennett; Rooney, Neal, McLean, Helm, Batty; Evans, Loupalo-Bi. Subs: Pradic, Andrew, Cannon, Davies, Thomas, Murphy, Coughlan, Robertson, Cirino.

Arsenal (4-2-3-1): Meslier; Timber, Mosquera, Salmon, Hincapie; Zubimendi, Lewis-Skelly; Dowman, Havertz, O’Neill; Gyokeres. Subs: Arrizabalaga, White, Odegaard, Eze, Konsa, Guimaraes, Hashi, Washington, Harriman-Annous.

To give everyone a flavour of conditions, it is blowing a gale around the rather quaint Highbury Stadium, almost blowing my laptop onto the pitch. Mikel Arteta will not be too worried about his players adapting to the conditions and will see a front pairing of Kai Havertz and Viktor Gyökeres as integral to killing this game off as soon as possible.

Fleetwood, however, are here to give the Gunners a bloody nose at the very least. They know how to utilise the conditions to their favour and will be straight into the faces of the Premier League champions. They may sit 18th in League Two, but they saw off Sheffield United in the last round and will fancy doing likewise in front of a packed ground.

Some sad news to bring you: Peter Dobing, the captain of Stoke’s 1972 League Cup winning side, has died at the age of 87.

Dobing made 377 appearances over a decade with the Potters and also played for Blackburn and Manchester City. He captained Stoke to glory as they beat Chelsea 2-1 to clinch their first (and only) major trophy, before his playing career was ended by injury just one year later.

Stoke said in a statement: “All at Stoke are deeply saddened to learn of the passing of our ’72 League Cup-winning captain Peter Dobing at the age of 87. The thoughts and sincere condolences of all are with Peter’s friends and family at this immensely difficult time.”

Some essential pre-match reading here from Will Unwin, our man on the Fylde coast tonight.

Some Arsenal fans spent years pining for one more game at Highbury Stadium; tonight, they finally get their wish. This particular Highbury is 250 miles northwest of Islington – the home of Fleetwood Town, a team the Gunners have never played before.

While their League Two form could hardly be less remarkable (18th place, with 14 points from 13 games), Fleetwood have picked off Chesterfield, Shrewsbury and second-tier Sheffield United in the Carabao Cup without conceding a goal, earning a last-16 glamour tie against the English champions.

With a trip to Anfield looming on Sunday, Mikel Arteta is expected to give his weariest stars a rest. Young talents like Marli Salmon, Ceadach O’Neill and, yes, Max Dowman are expected to start. They can expect testing conditions at a seaside ground where “the wind goes in all directions”.

Of course, even a depleted and under-experienced Arsenal side will be hot favourites to advance, but this has all the makings of a classic midweek Cup test. Kick-off: 8pm GMT.

#517. This AI-powered robot vacuum from Shark is almost $300 off at Amazon

SAVE $270: As of Oct. 27, the Shark AV2501S AI Ultra robot vacuum is on sale for $279.99 at Amazon. That's a 49% discount on the list price.

Staying on top of floor cleaning can feel like an endless task, especially if you have a busy home with pets that love to shed. Robot vacuums take some of that hassle away from you, and models like the Shark AV2501S AI Ultra can do so with incredible efficiency.

The vacuum comes with a bagless, self-emptying base that can hold up to 30 days' worth of dirt and debris, so you won't need to empty it after every cleaning session. And its self-cleaning brushroll is designed to tackle loose hair, so long strands or pet hair stand no chance.

Battery life is another useful feature, with up to 120 minutes of runtime on a full charge. When the battery runs low, the robot can return to its base to recharge and resume where it left off, so you don't have to restart the cleaning cycle yourself.

If this robot vacuum sounds like a dream come true for your chores list, you'll be pleased to know it's also on sale. As of Oct. 27, it's down to $279.99, saving you $270. You'll find it available at Amazon right now.

#516. Wagner brothers begin new NBA chapter on different paths

After five years, brothers Franz and Moritz Wagner are no longer playing together for the Orlando Magic. In the new NBA season, their separation will likely mean the new "German" team is the Charlotte Hornets.

In 2021, when Franz Wagner joined the Orlando Magic to start his NBA career, his older brother Moritz was already on the team. Franz, who is four years younger, was selected eighth overall in the draft by the team who had signed Moritz a few months earlier.

It's not just their joint appearances in the NBA that have since turned the "Wagners" into something of a brand in basketball. Just last year, the NBA had a regular-season game in Berlin, partly because of the Wagner brothers.

Moritz is a successful podcast host and often has his brother on the show. Both have their own Instagram accounts, each with around 300,000 followers. In 2024, the German public broadcaster ZDF aired a four-part TV documentary about the brothers, chronicling their basketball history and close relationship.

In recent years, they've also celebrated success with the German national team. In 2023, they became world champions together and were then at the Olympic Games in Paris a year later. In 2025, Franz also won the European Championship title, while Moritz, sidelined by injury, watched on as a TV analyst.

This summer, their time together came to an end. After Moritz wasn't offered a new contract in Orlando, he accepted an offer from the Brooklyn Nets. He now lives in New York with his girlfriend, Maggie McQuade, who works in marketing for the NBA.

"I've already spent a lot of time in New York. It's kind of like my home in America," Wagner said on his podcast, adding that it reminds him of where he's from. "As far as the US goes, Brooklyn is actually very close to Berlin."

The call from the Nets proved a stroke of luck. "When we hung up the phone and both agreed, we both cried. Because you kind of realize that this move feels right somehow," Wagner said. "We're just staying home."

The Nets are considered one of the weaker teams heading into the upcoming season, leaving Wagner a key force in providing stability and helping to develop younger players.

Franz is staying in Orlando. Alongside Paolo Banchero, Franz is one of the team's two stars. The 25-year-old is on a massive contract that runs through 2030 and will earn him a reported total of $224 million (€199 million).

With Sean Sweeney hired as their new head coach for the upcoming season, the Magic are hoping to finally advance past the first round of the playoffs, having lost at that stage in each of the last three seasons.

Success is expected to come from a faster style of basketball than has been played in Orlando in recent years. In the new system, Franz Wagner will take on the role of point guard more prominently than before — moving the ball up the court, creating his own shots, and driving to the basket with speed — much like he does for Germany.

"We want to play tough, play together, play it fast and enjoy playing together," Wagner said during a preseason interview. "We got a little bit more space for our guys, to get in the paint and make plays, for themselves and for others."

However, he now has to manage without his older brother.

"I think we're both a little sad his time here has come to an end," Franz Wagner said in an interview with sports news site ClutchPoints. "I think, though, we're both really grateful for the time we had [together]. To play with a sibling in the NBA for so many years is super special. So we're just grateful for that."

Now that the Wagners’ time together in Orlando is history, German basketball fans may have to adjust their allegiances a bit for the upcoming season.

While many Germans became fans of the Dallas Mavericks during the era of German basketball icon Dirk Nowitzki, and interest in the Orlando Magic has been particularly high in recent years, that interest may spread in the coming season. A total of eight Germans are under contract on an NBA team for the 2026/27 season:

While Hartenstein, who won the NBA championship with the Thunder in 2025, has another shot at the title, Hukporti, now in Philadelphia, tore his Achilles tendon in the first preseason game and will be out for the entire season. The Sixers have signed NBA legend LeBron James and are considered one of the title contenders.

Maxi Kleber, a reliable bench player for the past nine years, did not receive a new contract from the Los Angeles Lakers and is currently a free agent.

The Charlotte Hornets could then become Germany's new favorite NBA team, with Germany captain and NBA nomad Dennis Schröder now on the roster. In his 14th year in the NBA, the Hornets are already his 12th different team.

Playing alongside him will be two German rookies, highly-touted Hannes Steinbach and Christian Anderson Jr. The two newcomers know each other from the German U19 national team, with which they won the silver medal at the 2025 World Championship.

"I'm a veteran — I've been in the league for 14 years now, I've actually been with the team the longest — and of course I'm trying to guide these two young guys through their rookie season and make it as easy as possible for them,” Schröder recently said in an interview with RTL/Sky Sport.

"It's really cool to have someone like that here. Dennis, of course, has already played a lot of games in the NBA," Steinbach said. "You have someone there you can speak German with, who gives you a few tips based on what he sees during the game."

The Hornets are aiming to make the playoffs at the end of the season. Since they play in the Eastern Conference, just like Brooklyn and Orlando, there will be plenty of matchups against the other German players along the way.

This article was originally written in German.

#515. 9 Unique Apps to Try on the iPhone Duo

Despite seven years of Android folding phones, Apple's iPhone Duo launch has spurred developers to create quirky, weird, and fun apps that experiment with the folding design (leaving Android owners feeling like second-class citizens yet again).

I've reviewed almost every folding phone in the past decade, and my inbox has never seen this many app pitches from indie developers wanting to show off their app for Apple's first foldable.

Some of these apps aren't necessarily new but use the phone's hinge to offer fresh experiences. You'll also find a “Made for iPhone Duo” badge on apps that make best use of the larger screens on the App Store. From a game that has you closing the phone to grill a piece of toast to a clever way to memorize flash cards, here are nine unique apps to try on your new Apple handset.

Board games will have a field day on the iPhone Duo, and Battlefleet Duo is the best example. On the larger 7.6-inch screen, you get a sprawling canvas, but in tabletop mode, you can view your ships on the bottom screen and the enemy's fleet above. That's especially great if you have a friend with the iPhone Duo, as you can play local multiplayer and sit across from each other just like you would with the old-school Battleships. You can play two free battles every day, or unlock unlimited battles with a one-time $5 purchase—no ads or subscription.

Reminiscing about the long-lost days of the pre-smartphone era? Flipcorder offers simple pleasure, turning your iPhone Duo into a camcorder, so you can make new memories that feel like the old days. Open the app, and the outer display will show a “closed lens”—open the Duo to “take the cap off,” then start recording. The user interface is retro, and video clips have a filter to emulate VHS (it has a MiniDV filter too). I really like that you can view your “tapes” and everything is cataloged by day, so you can see 30 minutes of footage in one stitched-together clip.

As you’re recording, the person on the other end can see a preview of the video on the outer display—that kind of dual-display experience is only available to camera apps, so if you were hoping to read a book and have the outer display appear as the book cover, that's sadly not possible at the moment.

Flashcard apps are a dime a dozen, but nothing quite captures the physical experience of flipping around a flashcard like RectoVerso. This $13 app lets you import your own text—your classroom notes or even a PDF—and uses Apple Intelligence to automatically create flashcards on the subject matter. Alternatively, you can import decks from Anki, another popular flashcard app, or write your own. The magic is when you're actually studying. Don't know the answer? Slightly open the iPhone Duo, and the answer will gradually appear—fully open, and you get the full answer. That way you can bang your hand on the desk and say, “Ah, I totally knew that.”

Who doesn't love a rhythm game? Pop your iPhone Duo into a 90-degree angle and let the blocks in PianoFold reach the middle crease—that's when you tap the corresponding column. You need to time your taps perfectly, unless you enjoy listening to Bach get butchered in real time. There are 40 levels across eight chapters, and the game rates your performance (out of three stars) at the end of each song. You can retry as many times as you'd like. The first two chapters are free, though you can also play one song for free every day. For full access, you'll have to pony up $5. There are no ads and no subscription.

When I showed my wife Toastie, she remarked that it might be the first time she ever wanted an iPhone (she uses a Pixel). That's high praise! This delightful $3 app asks you to construct a sandwich on the iPhone Duo's inner screen. Each recipe has specific instructions you have to follow, and when you're done, you'll need to close the iPhone for a few seconds—however many the recipe says—to “grill” your sandwich. Open the phone back up, and it's ready to serve! That is, if you haven't burned it. There are only 10 recipes, so you'll probably get through this quickly. But hey, at least you can say you've used the world's most expensive toaster.

Want to focus? Or relax? Portal lets you play relaxing soundscapes—listen to a volcano's bubbling lava or waves crashing on a beach—but you can also see these sights on the Duo's 7.6-inch canvas, in tabletop mode, or on the outer screen in tent mode. It's very relaxing and an excellent companion for travelers who rely on a sound machine to fall asleep. You can also follow calming breath exercises, or set focus timers to get stuff done. The app boasts more than 100 vistas, from Iceland's black beaches to enchanting UK woodlands. The app is free, though you can only access three portals refreshed monthly. Full access costs $3 a month, $30 per year, or $100 for a lifetime membership.

If you like the idea of Portal, you might want to check out Fold Focus, though it's designed to help you get work done rather than relax. Close the Duo to focus on a task, and the pixel animation on the outer display will start building a city. Open your phone before the timer ends and everything will come crashing down.

Want to try a new calendar app? Maybe your current one isn't optimized for the Duo (ahem, Google Calendar). Try FirstSeed Planner. The developer has fully enabled Duo support, letting you view the calendar on one screen and a daily planner on the other. It supports drag-and-drop actions between the two halves of the screen, and I also love the space at the bottom for writing notes for the day. And when Apple enables stylus support on the Duo later this year, you can handwrite in FirstSeed, making your Duo feel more like a paper planner. It's free, but the Pro version costs $3.49 per month, $30 per year, or $90 for lifetime access. The paid versions unlock features like automatic backups and weather forecasts.

If you’re big on retro gaming, you’ve probably heard of Delta. The popular emulator just rolled out its biggest update yet, headlined by support for the folding iPhone Duo. Prop the Duo at a 90-degree angle, and custom skins transform the device into a classic clamshell like the Nintendo DS—complete with automatic sleep mode when you close it. Just remember: It’s strictly an emulator, so you’ll still need to source your own ROMs responsibly.

Bring your favorite PCs and Macs back to life with Tiny Computers. This $4 app houses a library of 10 computers, from the Commodore 64 to the iMac G3. Fold the Duo into tabletop mode to bring the museum of machines to life—a vintage screen up top and a functional, clickable keyboard below. The operating systems are interactive, so you can run retro paint apps, secret terminal commands, or kill time with classic desktop games like Solitaire.

#514. New evidence suggests spinosaurus spent most of its life underwater

For millions of years, evolution helped animals make the transition from life in the ocean to life on land. But the journey did not always move in one direction. Many species eventually returned to the water, developing new adaptations that allowed them to thrive in aquatic environments.

Dozens of major groups of mammals and reptiles made this remarkable evolutionary reversal. Now, a new study from Yale University is shedding light on when and how these transitions occurred, as well as which ancient animals became fully aquatic and which continued to depend on land.

The findings were published in the journal Current Biology.

"These secondarily aquatic groups adapted in strikingly similar ways to their new aquatic home -- evolving flippers and a suite of other features that made them better swimmers," said lead author Caleb Gordon, who completed his doctorate at Yale's Graduate School of Arts and Sciences (GSAS) earlier this year and is now a postdoctoral researcher at the Florida Museum of Natural History. "As a result, they've become textbook examples of convergent evolution, which can tell us a lot about the processes driving and constraining adaptive change in response to similar environmental cues."

Using Ancient Fossils and AI to Reconstruct Evolution

To investigate these evolutionary changes, the researchers examined hundreds of fossil and modern specimens housed at the Yale Peabody Museum and dozens of other institutions worldwide. They collected extensive new data, including measurements (more than 11,000), photographs, and CT scans.

The team combined traditional paleontological techniques with machine learning models that account for evolutionary relationships. They also incorporated a statistical approach originally used by naval forces during World War II. Together, these methods offered a new way to investigate one of the most remarkable transitions in vertebrate evolution.

"Reconstructing the lives of extinct life forms in a scientifically rigorous way, as opposed to just telling stories, is a precise and delicate undertaking," said Bhart-Anjan Bhullar, senior author of the study. Bhullar is an associate professor of Earth and planetary sciences in Yale's Faculty of Arts and Sciences (FAS) and an associate curator of vertebrate paleontology and vertebrate zoology at the Peabody Museum.

"It requires a careful interweaving of data from modern organisms, of which our understanding is necessarily far deeper, and knowledge of these living organisms' genealogy relative to fossil forms," he said. "Caleb's work accomplishes all of this and more, and on a massive evolutionary scale."

For decades, scientists have disagreed about when particular groups of prehistoric animals became fully adapted to life underwater. The fossil record often provides incomplete or conflicting clues, making it difficult to determine whether an extinct species primarily lived on land, in water, or in both environments.

Some skeletal features may indicate an aquatic lifestyle, while others suggest that the same animal remained capable of moving and feeding on land. These contradictions have led researchers to different conclusions about how ancient species behaved.

"In these cases, paleontologists are often stuck, as different lines of evidence disagree about what the ancient animal was like," Gordon said.

To address this uncertainty, Gordon developed a method using relatively simple but effective machine learning models trained on living animals. By identifying relationships between skeletal proportions and known lifestyles, the models can estimate how extinct species used aquatic environments. They can also predict soft tissue adaptations, including swimming structures that rarely survive in fossils.

Spinosaurus May Have Spent Most of Its Life Underwater

One of the most striking findings concerns Spinosaurus, a giant prehistoric dinosaur whose relationship with water has generated considerable controversy.

Spinosaurus lived approximately 113 million to 94 million years ago in what is now northern Africa. Although scientists generally agree that it was associated with aquatic environments, exactly how it moved, hunted, and spent its time remains a subject of debate.

Some evidence points to an animal that frequently submerged itself and pursued prey underwater, much like seals or penguins. Other findings suggest a lifestyle closer to that of a modern heron, with Spinosaurus walking and searching for food along shorelines and in shallow water.

The Yale team's analysis provides new support for the more aquatic interpretation.

"Our results shed new light on how much time Spinosaurus spent submerged, which could support the underwater hunting view," Gordon said. "We confidently recovered highly aquatic habits for Spinosaurus, indicating that it spent the vast majority of its time submerged in the water."

Although the findings do not settle every question about how Spinosaurus hunted, they strengthen the case that this enormous predator spent much more of its life underwater than on land.

Ancient Marine Reptiles That Never Fully Left Land

The researchers reached a different conclusion about mesosaurs, small marine reptiles that lived between 290 million and 274 million years ago in regions now forming parts of South Africa and eastern South America.

Mesosaurs have long interested paleontologists because they gave birth to live young, a characteristic some researchers have interpreted as evidence of a fully aquatic existence. However, the new analysis suggests that their limbs were not adapted for a life spent entirely in water.

Instead, their skeletal proportions indicate that mesosaurs regularly moved between aquatic and terrestrial environments.

"They spent a lot of time on land, like a modern-day alligator or platypus does," Gordon said. "Mesosaurs didn't completely leave the land behind."

The findings also suggest a broader pattern among the earliest marine reptiles. According to the researchers, all Paleozoic marine reptiles (all marine reptiles around before the dinosaurs appeared) continued to return to land regularly. None appeared to have evolved a completely aquatic lifestyle, with even the most water adapted species remaining amphibious.

The researchers found that forelimb proportions were particularly useful for distinguishing these lifestyles. Using this information, their models could predict with more than 90% accuracy whether an animal possessed soft tissue flippers and whether it had highly or fully aquatic habits.

A World War II Technique Helps Solve an Evolutionary Mystery

An especially unusual part of the research involved adapting a statistical method developed during the 1940s.

"Caleb resurrected a statistical technique from a bygone era," said co-author Jacques Gauthier, a professor of Earth and planetary sciences at Yale and curator-in-charge for vertebrate paleontology at the Peabody Museum. "It was developed during WWII to estimate the probability that an approaching blip on the radar screen was an enemy plane. Caleb cleverly used it to get at whether or not an extinct animal had limbs modified for swimming."

By repurposing this approach, the researchers could assess the likelihood that an extinct species possessed specialized swimming adaptations, even when direct evidence of those features was missing.

The method could eventually help scientists investigate other major changes in evolutionary history. Potential applications include studying when human ancestors developed the ability to walk on two legs and how the dinosaur ancestors of birds evolved the ability to fly.

Because most prehistoric vertebrates are known primarily through their fossilized skeletons, reconstructing their behavior is one of paleontology's greatest challenges. Bones preserve valuable information, but they rarely provide a complete picture of how an animal lived.

Traditionally, scientists have interpreted particular skeletal features to make arguments about the lifestyles of extinct species. Those interpretations can differ significantly, especially when fossils are incomplete or several anatomical characteristics point toward different conclusions.

The new approach offers a more systematic way to evaluate that evidence, using patterns observed in living species to make testable predictions about animals that disappeared millions of years ago.

"Bones are all that is left to us of the vast majority of vertebrate animals that have lived on Earth," Bhullar said. "We want to know so very much about these creatures and the succession of lost worlds they inhabited, but we have so little on which to base our conclusions. From skeletal remains, people have tried to discern life habits, generally making an argument on the basis of one preferred set of features or another. Caleb's method removes the subjectivity from this process and allows the data to speak for themselves."

The study's additional co-authors are Lisa Freisem, a doctoral student at Yale's Graduate School of Arts and Sciences, and Christopher Griffin, an assistant professor of geosciences at Princeton University who previously worked as a postdoctoral fellow at Yale.

Research support was provided by the Yale Institute for Biospheric Studies, the International Union for Conservation of Nature's Crocodile Specialist Group, and the National Science Foundation.

Materials provided by Yale University. Original written by Jim Shelton. Note: Content may be edited for style and length.

#513. Broadway Box Office: Billy Crystal’s ‘860’ Sees Strong Opening, As ‘Downstate’ and More Start Previews

'Other Desert Cities' with Julia Louis-Dreyfus and Allison Janney is still proving a starry draw.

Two more shows began previews last week, with several more on deck, as Broadway‘s fall season heats up.

Hamilton, with original cast member Christopher Jackson back on stage, remained the highest grossing show last week bringing in $2.5 million, followed by Other Desert Cities, with $2.3 million. The play, starring Julia Louis-Dreyfus, Allison Janney and Joe Keery, retained the highest average ticket price on Broadway, at $290, and played to 100 percent capacity, after opening Oct. 18 and receiving mixed reviews.

Harry Potter and the Cursed Child was the third highest grossing with $1.99 million, The Lion King with $1.9 million and MJ with $1.7 million. Related Stories Lifestyle Jonathan Groff to Return to Broadway in Solo Play 'The Amish Project' Lifestyle Tom Hanks Sets Broadway Return in 'This World of Tomorrow'

Billy Crystal’s solo show 860 opened on Oct. 21 to largely positive reviews, with capacity for the seven performances at around 98 percent. The show still brought in $1.2 million, despite a comped opening night, and has announced an extension through Feb. 14.

After a delayed first preview, Downstate, produced by Scott Rudin, played its first six performances last week to 95 percent capacity. The play, which opens Nov. 15, brought in just $264,437 with an average ticket price of $61.64, suggesting some deep discounts.

New musical Wanted played its first full week of eight performances, continuing with strong capacity of 93 percent, albeit a slight dip from the prior week. The show brought in $624,088 across the eight performances, as it continues to implement discounted preview pricing.

The revival of The Fanstasticks also saw strong attendance for its first four performances last week, wiht 100 percent capacity at the Helen Hayes Theater, owned by the nonprofit Second Stage. The musical grossed $254,610.

A Few Good Men, starring Bradley Whitford and Tom Blyth, is next up to open, on Oct. 29. The play has been seeing strong grosses and attendance, with close to $1.4 million last week.

#510. Which of This Week’s Big Chart Debuts — From Tate McRae, Olivia Rodrigo & Chappell Roan and Zach Bryan & Lana Del Rey — Will Stick Around Longest?

This week's Five Burning Questions looks at a suddenly crowded week near the top of the Billboard Hot 100.

After a couple slower weeks for movement in the top tier of the Billboard Hot 100, big things are going down on this week’s chart (dated Oct. 31), thanks to a handful of new releases from some major hitmakers. Explore Tate McRae See latest videos, charts and news

The week’s highest debut comes from still-rising hitmaker Tate McRae, who lands a new career high as a lead artist on the chart with the No. 2 bow of her new single “Housewife.” It’s joined in the Hot 100’s top five by Olivia Rodrigo’s “Expectations” — which had previously peaked at No. 20 on the chart in its solo version, but now bounds 63-3 thanks to a new remix featuring Chappell Roan — and by Zach Bryan’s new Lana Del Rey-featuring “Lies,” which enters at No. 4. (All three, of course, come just short of passing Ella Langley’s “Choosin’ Texas,” which returns for a 25th week atop the chart.) Related Ryan Gosling's Dead Man's Bones Duo Creep Back With Romantic 'Lost in the Night' Video Starring Noah Jupe and Rain Spencer Taylor Swift, George Strait, Lee Thomas Miller & More Celebrated During Nashville Songwriters Hall of Fame Gala Disturbed to Play 'The Sickness' in Full on Australia and New Zealand Tour

Which of the new top five hits is most likely to keep contending into the new year? And will anything else unseat “Texas” this year before the holiday rush hits? Billboard staffers answer these questions and more below.

1. Tate McRae’s “Housewife” debuts at No. 2 this week, already making for McRae’s highest-charting song as a debut artist. On a scale from 1-10, how big a deal would you say this entrance is for her?

Christopher Claxton: It’s a big deal since she beat out new releases from Taylor Swift, Olivia Rodrigo and some of music’s biggest names. I’ll give this moment a 8 out of 10 — but it may deserve an even-higher score, considering that while “Housewife” landed at No. 2 on the Hot 100, it debuted at No. 1 on both of Billboard’s global charts.

I think this shows how an artist can make a major impact even without landing at No. 1. “Housewife” is already connecting with listeners around the world, and between its global chart debuts and its Hot 100 performance, Tate is starting this era with real gas.

Hannah Dailey: I’ll say 8 and save my 10 for when she debuts at No. 1 – but the runner-up slot is still fantastic, and shows how much heat she’s packing as a pop it-girl going into this next era.

Joe Lynch: 9. I could even be persuaded to say 10 – after all, if not for the unprecedented blockbuster that is “Choosin’ Texas,” McRae would have scored her first No. 1 as a solo artist, and that’s major. Regardless, she debuted at No. 1 on both Billboard Global charts. This is a huge moment – her pop star power is no longer something to be debated or qualified but accepted.

Michael Saponara: 9. This really couldn’t have gone much better for Tate McRae. She releases “Housewife” and delivers the live debut of the single at the Victoria’s Secret Fashion Show, where she was born to perform on the runway. It’s her highest Hot 100 debut yet and was the most streamed song in the country. She and Ryan Tedder continue to build on one of the most potent artist-producer relationships in pop. Whenever there’s a ceiling seemingly placed on Tate, she drives her sports car right through it. The only reason this isn’t a 10 is because she just missed out on unseating Ella Langley.

Andrew Unterberger: I think a 7.5 is reasonable. A No. 2 peak is hardly a new stratosphere for an artist who’s already had two No. 3s (“Greedy” and “Tit for Tat”), but outpacing such starry competition and giving “Choosin’ Texas” enough of a scare that chart-watchers were on the edge of their seat for the final couple days of the tracking week… it just proves that McRae’s really running with the big dogs of pop music now.

2. Just below “Housewife,” Olivia Rodrigo and Chappell Roan’s “Expectations” jumps from 63-3 in the first full week of the new remix’s release. Is that No. 3 landing spot higher, lower or about what you would’ve expected for the all-star team-up?

Christopher Claxton: Honestly it’s about where I expected. We had a stacked week with drops from Taylor Swift, Zach Bryan with Lana Del Rey and Tate of course. So many huge artists with even larger fanbases releasing at once makes chart positions a bit difficult. But they made more progress than we might realize, 63 to 3 is a huge jump and that’s definitely something to celebrate.

Hannah Dailey: These are two of the biggest names in pop in 2026, and all of us are starved for any sort of new Roan material. It makes sense to me that it debuted in this position and is about what I expected, maybe even slightly higher.

Joe Lynch: So you’re asking about my expectations for “Expectations”? I was anticipating a top 5 jump given the Chappell-Olivia dream team and No. 3 is squarely in the middle of that – it’s a great placement for this silly, unserious (in a fun way) song. Roan adds a level of defiant camp to this song that makes it make so much more sense than the original IMO.

Michael Saponara: Initially it was higher than I expected for a song that’s been out for more than four months, but when you get a pair of Gen-Z’s pop titans colliding on a remix, who are also good friends, the vault to the top five makes sense.

Andrew Unterberger: No. 3 feels perfect, honestly. I know a lot of pop fans were holding out hope for a No. 1 — I won’t pretend I wasn’t one — but I never actually believed it was too likely; no matter how star-studded or well-considered a remix may be, it’s still a remix, and far more likely to boost an already-established megahit than generate a totally new one. And considering this is now one of the best-performing songs from one one of the best-performing albums of the year, I’d say the “Expectations” remix still more than did its job there. 3. Zach Bryan’s “Lies” No. 4 debut alongside Lana Del Rey marks his second (of two) top five Hot 100 hits to feature a big-name female duet partner, following the Kacey Musgraves-featuring, Hot 100-topping “I Remember Everything” in 2024. If Bryan wants to go three-for-three there, who should his third teamup be with?

Christopher Claxton: If Bryan wants to go three-for-three with female duet partners on the Hot 100, I think he should tap in with Megan Moroney. The two haven’t collaborated on a song yet, but they already have a connection: Megan joined Bryan for a performance of “Revival” in 2023 and has covered his music in the past. Moroney has had 15 songs appear on the Hot 100 but hasn’t scored a top five hit yet. Maybe the Emo Cowgirl is just the partner Bryan needs to make it a 3-peat.

Hannah Dailey: How about Tate McRae? If she topped the Hot 100 with Bryan, everyone might finally forget the time she did so with another more controversial country star – not to mention the fact that she’s a total force right now, and would elevate the profile of any track she guests on.

Joe Lynch: If we’re talking about concocting a can’t-miss-the-top 5 team-up, I would say Ella Langley is the obvious choice, and I would imagine they could cook up some compelling in-studio chemistry. But I feel like Lainey Wilson and Bryan could really cut loose together, too.

Michael Saponara: The first name that popped in my head was Ella Langley, whose chart dominance in 2026 speaks for itself, and that country combination is a sure-fire bet for commercial success.

Andrew Unterberger: The performers Bryan seems to have the most success with are the ones who have a foot in both the country and the pop worlds, with a little bit of an alt edge to them. So with that in mind, I’d advise him to eschew the slightly starrier names and enlist Avery Anna, who’s already had Hot 100 duet success both with another alt-skewing country star (“Indigo” with Sam Barber) and with an actual rock band (“Why Don’t You Say So” with Dexter and the Moonrocks). It’d be a natural fit, and probably a great look for both artists.

4. Of the three new top five hits, which would you say is the most likely to still be in the mix for the top spot well into 2027?

Christopher Claxton: I think Tate’s “Housewife” could have some staying power. When the track dropped it made an immediate impact, earning 55.9 million global streams and 6,000 sales worldwide during its first tracking week. That 55.9 million number is already very high, but I feel like there’s still room to grow, especially if she continues to perform it live like, she did at the 2026 Victoria’s Secret Fashion Show.

Hannah Dailey: I think I’m going to say “Lies.” McRae’s single has the benefit of being a brand-new song as opposed to a remix of an already released track, while Rodrigo and Roan have the advantage of a bill with two big names rather than one. “Lies” has both. It’s also way more palatable for laid-back listening across genres and generations.

Joe Lynch: Tate’s for sure. The Rodrigo/Roan remix is delightful and will likely continue to be a strong streamer, but I don’t see it sticking around on radio through 2027 (likely another Olivia single will be out by then). “Lies” is a great duet but seems like it will drop out of the top 10 after a week or two, and it’s unlikely to become a mainstream radio mainstay. I could see it becoming a long-running Adult Alternative Airplay No. 1, though.

Michael Saponara: It’s gotta be “Housewife,” which feels like it has legit staying power and should be dominating radio airwaves shortly. The stage is set for Tate McRae to make another leap in the pop pantheon with her fourth album on the horizon for quarter one ‘27 and “Housewife” should remain a chart staple. My only worry for T8: Ryan Tedder said “Housewife” closes the book on McRae’s 2000s-era pop sound that she’s carefully sculpted, and fans should expect a sonic pivot when it’s album time.

Andrew Unterberger: Yeah, Tate ain’t even close to done yet. “Housewife” was ticking backup on streaming by the end of its first release week, and is still topping the daily listings on both Spotify and Apple Music, while also picking up steam on radio by the day. Not only does it feel like a safe bet for the first big stable chart hit of 2027, I also wouldn’t be surprised if it ended up going to No. 1 for the first time after the holidays — assuming “Choosin’ Texas” ever decides to start losing steam, anyway.

5. Prediction time: Will there be another No. 1 in between “Choosin’ Texas” and the holiday takeover in 2026?

Christopher Claxton: I unfortunately don’t see that happening unless Taylor Swift has a few more songs up her sleeve or we get a surprise drop from Beyoncé.

Hannah Dailey: Yes – it’ll take a real pre-Christmas miracle, but yes.

Joe Lynch: The easy (and safest) answer is to say no. But I’ll roll the dice and say sure – maybe Halloween pushes “Dracula” to the top spot, maybe Swift swings a return to No. 1, maybe McRae’s “Housewife” manages to lease out the top spot until Carey sets up shop at No. 1 for the holidays.

Andrew Unterberger: Yes, but only because I’m a Halloween No. 1 truther.

#509. Dysons Supersonic Nural is nearly $80 off, but is this viral hair dryer worth $372?

SAVE OVER $70: As of Oct. 27, the Dyson Supersonic Nural is on sale for $372.49 at Amazon. That's a 17% discount on the list price.

Every day should be a good hair day, and while the best dupes can go a long way, there's nothing quite like the real thing when it comes to the Dyson Supersonic Nural. Its clever technology doesn't just give you perfectly blown-out hair, but it also protects it from heat damage that's normally associated with hot styling.

As of Oct. 27, the Dyson Supersonic Nural is on sale for more than $70 off the list price. Usually $449.99, it's now $372.49 at Amazon.

This hair dryer is known for its built-in sensor technology, which automatically adjusts settings to help make drying more comfortable. Scalp Protect mode, for example, regulates the air temperature to maintain a target of 130°F at the scalp, helping to reduce exposure to excessive heat.

This Dyson dryer also learns your styling preferences. The attachment recognition feature identifies which accessory you're using and automatically remembers your preferred heat and airflow settings, so you don't have to adjust them manually every time. There's also Pause Detect, which reduces airflow and switches off the heater when you put the dryer down, then turns it back on when you use it again.

This Dyson deal is available at Amazon now, so don't miss out.

#508. United Airlines set to open its biggest airport lounge in $2.6 billion overhaul at Houston hub

United Airlines is set to open its biggest airport lounge this week, which it says is the largest in the Americas, as airlines continue to plow money into the facilities for high-spending customers.

The 54,000-square-foot lounge is opening Thursday at George Bush Intercontinental Airport's Terminal B in Houston, part of an overhaul of the facility that includes 22 gates for mainline aircraft.

The airport, with more than 500 departures a day, is United's main connecting hub for flights to and from Latin America.

The new club can seat 800 people and features an outdoor terrace for plane-spotting as well as two full-service bars and buffets.

The lounge, part of United's $2.6 billion investment at the airport's Terminal B, shows how airlines are putting more money into larger spaces for premium-travel demand to attract the growing ranks of elite frequent flyers, co-branded credit card holders and customers just willing to spend more to fly.

American Airlines earlier this month said it is planning its largest-ever lounge at its Charlotte Douglas International Airport hub in North Carolina. Delta Air Lines has expanded both its popular Delta Sky Clubs and its top-tier Delta One Lounges.

Southwest Airlines is building its first-ever network of airport lounges, which are tied to its credit card partner, Chase. Last week, the carrier unveiled mock-ups of the lounges, which it is calling Icon. And JetBlue Airways opened lounges in New York and Boston in the past year.

#507. Germany news: Hackers release AfD party emails

Germany's far-right AfD has launched an inquiry after the release of thousands of emails following a cyberattack. Also, investors pledged billions for Germany at a major summit in Berlin. DW has more news from Germany.

This blog is now closed. Below is a round-up of top stories from and about Germany on Tuesday, October 20, 2026.

Alice Weidel, the co-leader of Germany's far-right AfD, is expected to present her party's policy for the EU at the European Parliament on Wednesday.

According to a report from news outlet Politico, the AfD policy paper on the EU does not seek to exit the bloc or the eurozone at first.

The party rather wants to downsize the EU's powers and budget.

Instead of direct European Parliament elections, the AfD would like to see delegates appointed by national parliaments. The AfD would also limit the scope of the European Commission to purely administrative tasks and supplant the European Court of Justice with an arbitration tribunal.

The AfD would threaten to slash Germany's contributions to the EU budget.

Germany under the AfD would seek exemptions from EU regulations regarding issues such as migration and social policy. The AfD may also seek opt-outs of other areas such as agriculture and financial policies.

If the EU would not bend to the AfD's demands for reform, the party would then seek a German referendum on EU membership.

A referendum would be unrealistic, as it would require a constitutional amendment to the German Basic Law to take place. An amendment would require a two-thirds majority vote in both the Bundestag and upper house Bundesrat — an extremely high threshold to meet.

The AfD hopes that it can implement its EU policies in 2029, when the next German and European elections will take place.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

Recent German federal election surveys show the AfD in first place but it would have to form a coalition with another partner to establish a ruling majority government. That's difficult when other parties such as the conservative Christian Democratic Union refuse to have any cooperation with the AfD as part of a "firewall" policy.

More than 421,000 emails linked to Germany's far-right AfD have been published following a cyberattack (see additional entry below).

The alleged material includes private communications, sensitive personal data and reported references to racism, antisemitism and Nazi ideology, although much of the trove has not yet been independently verified.

Could this become one of the biggest political data leaks in modern German history?

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

A German supermarket is getting attention online after responding to a customer who objected to its diverse workforce.

Nine pro-Palestinian protesters seeking an end to Western arms exports to Israel glued themselves to the ground in front of a Berlin facility belonging to German weapons manufacturer Rheinmetall.

Berlin police responded to the incident at 5:30 a.m. (0330 UTC) with 11 protesters at the site. The "Peacefully Against Genocide" group was behind the action.

The factory began arms production in June. It is involved in the production of shell casings for artillery ammunition and parts for kamikaze drones, according to local public broadcaster RBB.

It is unclear how much the facility in Berlin's Wedding neighborhood is involved with weapons for Israel. Rheinmetall, however, is Germany's largest arms manufacturer.

In the western German state of Hesse, seven people also blocked the entry to the Rheinmetall facility in the city of Kassel, according to local police.

In addition, demonstrators against weapons exports glued themselves in front of a weapons company in the western city of Kaiserslautern.

Germany's far-right Alternative for Germany (AfD) said on Tuesday that it was looking into the theft and subsequent online publication of emails stolen by unidentified hackers during a cyberattack last month.

An AfD spokesperson said the hackers had targeted a hosting provider used by the party and appeared to have copied data from a small number of mostly inactive email accounts.

"The recent online publication of what is presumably a portion of this data is currently being investigated," the spokesperson said. "A preliminary assessment can only be provided once this investigation has been concluded."

Although the spokesperson did not say how much data was stolen, an earlier report by the mass-circulation daily Bild put the number of AfD-linked emails published at more than 420,000.

According to Bild, the material published included not only emails sent by party figures such as co-leader Alice Weidel but also bank statements, academic certificates and membership lists.

The website hosting the material was offline by Tuesday afternoon.

However, archived versions showed a volume of 281 gigabytes of data under the headline "The party's own records, unedited."

The emails and other materials published have not yet been certified as authentic by any independent body.

The AfD, several state chapters of which have been classified as "confirmed right-wing extremist" by regional branches of Germany's domestic intelligence agency, has been topping recent national opinion polls and made gains in recent state elections.

Some politicians and civil society groups have called for it to be banned, arguing that the party is a threat to democracy, an accusation the party has rejected.

While Berlin hosts a summit on investment in Germany, an increasing amount of US capital is flowing into Germany's traditional industrial firms and family-owned businesses.

As many business owners struggle to find successors, more companies are becoming acquisition targets.

Read all the details here: Why US investors are snapping up Germany's famed Mittelstand

German economic confidence — like that across the eurozone in general — deteriorated in October, according to experts at the Mannheim-based Center for European Economic Research (ZEW).

The ZEW's monthly economic confidence index fell to a three-month low on Tuesday, down to 28.1 from 34.7 in September. That is nearly two points lower than forecast.

"Private consumption and investment remain weak because of high energy prices: They put real incomes under pressure, increase production costs and keep inflation higher for longer," said ZEW President Achim Wambach.

"The German economy," added Wambach, "is slowly emerging from stagnation, but the recovery is not self-sustaining."

The biggest losers in this latest index were banks, insurers, the service sector and the telecommunications industry.

Winners were seen in the metal manufacturing and electronics industries.

ZEW compiles its monthly surveys from interviews with banking, insurance and financial industry experts and businesses on their expectations for economic and capital market developments.

The ZEW's index is considered a key indicator for Germany's economic development.

Tuesday's survey was based on interviews with 178 analysts between October 12 and 19, 2026.

Chancellor Friedrich Merzhas indulged in an abundance of the most common cliches about his country and compatriots while trying to portray Germany as the ideal place to do business at an investment summit.

"Germans are hardworking, going to bed early, rising before dawn," he told the domestic and international guests at the Invest in Germany Summit.

"Worst traffic is in the morning between 6 and 7," he said.

"On holidays, we are first on the beach, spreading towels before breakfast. If we agree on delivery dates, we stick to it. If you invite friends from Germany for dinner at 7, we will arrive at 6:58.

"We are reliable, predictable, precise, and unsurprising," the chancellor continued. "We are, in short, what investors like most."

Finance Minister Lars Klingbeil also used blunt humor in his appeal to potential investors, saying in his speech simply: "I want your money."

Both pitches appear to have succeeded, with investment commitments worth billions made at the summit.

More than €225 billion ($252.5 billion) has so far been pledged in investments in Germany at a summit in Berlin attended by top business leaders, German Economy Minister Katherina Reiche said on Tuesday.

Reiche did not give any details about the commitments made so far, but told broadcaster Deutschlandfunk that the figure only included deals above a volume of €100 million.

Among those planning to invest in Europe's largest economy is online retail giant Amazon, which said it intended to pour some €16 billion into Germany next year for various schemes.

The company said these would include "the expansion of data centers and AI infrastructure, high-speed satellite internet through Amazon Leo, new logistics projects across the country, and a major new production slate by Prime Video."

"Germany is a hub of innovation and one of the most attractive investment opportunities in Europe," David Zapolsky, Amazon's chief global affairs and legal officer, was quoted as saying in a statement.

"That is why we have invested more than €100 billion here since 2010, and why we plan to invest an additional €16 billion in 2027. We want German businesses, communities, and creators to have the infrastructure and tools they need to compete and grow."

More than 200 German and international executives from large corporations, investment banks and funds are attending the gathering, entitled the Invest in Germany Summit.

The Left Party in Berlin, which has faced accusations of being antisemitic, is holding a meeting to discuss how to deal with its parliamentarian Vanessa Emde, who has raised controversy with pro-Palestinian statements.

Among other things, Emde has supported the idea that there is no right to exist for states, but only for people — thus seemingly going against Germany's firm support of Israel's right to existence.

Emde has refused to retract any of her statements.

She has also criticized a statement on antisemitism that she had signed along with all other members of the Left's parliamentary party in which the signatories committed to upholding Israel's right to existence.

This comes as both the Berlin Green Party and the Social Democrats (SPD) confirmed that they were ready to begin coalition negotiations with the Left Party on forming a joint government in the city-state.

The Left Party is due to decide on the issue on October 31 at a party conference.

The three parties held preliminary discussions on the accusations of antisemitism and links to organized crime leveled at the Left Party before beginning the exploratory talks that precede final coalition negotiations.

The Left Party emerged from September 20 elections in Berlin as the strongest party with 25.7% of the vote.

Both the Greens (14.3%) and the center-left SPD (12.1%) came in behind the conservative Christian Democrats (18.8%), who led a previous coalition in Berlin with the SPD.

The far-right Alternative for Germany (AfD) received 16.3% of the vote, but so far, all other parties are officially refusing to enter into any form of cooperation with it at either federal or state level.

The Invest in Germany Summit in Berlin entered its second and final day on Tuesday after opening the previous evening with a speech by Chancellor Friedrich Merz that included the words: "Germany is open for business."

The summit has already yielded an agreement to deepen cooperation on artificial intelligence (AI) in German industry between Germany and US tech giant Nvidia.

Merz and Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang said the combination of Germany's engineering expertise and cutting-edge AI technology would help strengthen the country's economy over the long term.

More than 200 German and international executives representing large corporations, investment banks and funds are attending the event, with closed-door talks scheduled for Tuesday.

The summit aims to attract foreign investment to Germany. Organizers highlight the country's sound public finances, triple-A credit rating as the only G7 nation to hold one, strong rule of law and leading position in European patent filings.

Despite these advantages, Germany has struggled to attract foreign investment in recent years, with high energy prices and labor costs acting as deterrents.

Government sources say investment will be vital to help bridge gaps in public funding as the country seeks to improve infrastructure and support companies' transition to climate-neutral production.

The summit is also being attended by Economy Minister Katherina Reiche and Finance Minister Lars Klingbeil, who also serves as Merz's deputy.

Nine protesters have glued themselves to the road outside a plant run by defense contractor Rheinmetall in Berlin's Wedding district, police said on Tuesday.

Officers have been trying since the early morning to detach the group, a police spokeswoman said.

She said as many as 90 officers had been deployed to the site, where altogether 11 protesters were gathered.

Rheinmetall is Germany's largest weapons manufacturer.

The plant in Wedding, where production began earlier this year, has been the target of several protests, with climate and social justice activist Greta Thunberg among those participating.

German industrial giant Siemens has warned against eliminating entry-level jobs that are now increasingly left to artificial intelligence (AI), saying young workers could be left without essential skills, including those needed to monitor AI-generated work.

Judith Wiese, the company's chief people and sustainability officer, said that handing certain routine tasks to AI meant that young employees would not gain hands-on experience that could benefit them later in their careers.

Wiese said it was necessary, for example, for software developers to be able to write their own code so they could critically examine the code produced by AI.

"The crucial question is always: What do I need to have done, checked and seen myself to avoid being misled by AI?" she said.

However, Wiese said AI could also help employees acquire skills more quickly, while older colleagues could learn from younger ones who had grown up using AI.

Wiese said it was still too early to say how AI would impact employment.

Noting that Siemens has some 30,000 software engineers, she said: "You can use the additional productivity either to do the same work with fewer people or to do more with the same number of employees."

Wiese also pointed out that AI was speeding up work processes, enabling the exploration of new ideas and creating projects that would not have been possible without it.

DW''s newsroom in Bonn bids a warm guten Tag to all its readers as a previously balmy October decides to move toward more wintry chill.

As Chancellor Merz hosts an investment conference in Berlin where developments in AI is taking center stage, industrial giant Siemens has warned that the technology does not only bring advantages.

The controversy over alleged antisemitism in Berlin's Left Party, which might well soon lead a leftist coalition in the city-state, continues, with the party itself debating what to do about a member who has expressed strong anti-Israel views.

And people in Germany are likely to face much longer waits for doctors' appointments from next year, according to an organization managing family doctors and therapists across Germany.
